OpenSUSE Leap 16.1

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The openSUSE project has announced the release of openSUSE Leap 16.1. The release is notable for allowing both traditional installations and immutable images with transactional updates. While immutable images had previously been available for Leap Micro, they are now available across all platforms that Leap supports, including servers, desktops, and virtual machines.

'We don't have enough time to recover:' Is the NBA game outpacing the players?

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Standing near half-court with a towel draped over his shoulders, Thunder center Isaiah Hartenstein attempted to drum up encouragement for his rookie deputy Aday Mara as he approached the bench. Teammates Micah Peavy and Bennett Stirtz waited to do the same, while occasionally tilting their heads back at the jumbotron, which flashed a deficit of nearly 20 points. Veteran guard Alex Caruso, dressed in street clothes, could only clap his hands in hopes of bringing a jolt of energy that simply would not come in a 119-98 loss to the Rockets — an unimaginable, disheartening new normal.

The 2026-27 season kicked off a fortnight ago, but Oklahoma City has already endured a campaign's worth of misfortune. Jalen Williams, lost to a fractured left fibula and a dislocated ankle. Cason Wallace, lost to a torn left labrum. Chet Holmgren, lost to a right patellar tendon tear. A tight-knit group that has always preached the importance of being where their feet are now is struggling to find its footing. A team that stood atop basketball's pinnacle less than 18 months ago now is dealing with questions about ever seriously challenging again, despite still boasting the reigning MVP and a former Coach of the Year. It's a unique type of hoops purgatory the organization hasn't experienced before.

"The morale's been pretty good all things considered," coach Mark Daigneault said on Saturday. "A week ago, the circumstances were a lot different than they are today. We understand that. We try to have an approach that can transcend these circumstances and the guys have done a great job. Obviously, the challenge is a little different now than it was prior to those injuries, but we have to recalibrate to what the challenge is and attack it a day and a game at a time."

While injuries have always been a part of the game, especially when competing at the highest level of the sport, the widespread volume and degree of ailments is troubling. One team being suddenly down 60% of its projected starting lineup is one thing, albeit a needle-in-a-haystack type probability. But the list of unavailables — Anthony Edwards, Tyrese Haliburton, Ja Morant, Ben Simmons, Jaren Jackson Jr., Paolo Banchero, Jalen Duren, Kawhi Leonard, Jalen Suggs and others — is growing, with various timelines ranging from multiple weeks to multiple months. Stars and teams are also being cautious with back-to-backs, whether due to early load management or having returned from long-term injuries. (The Pacers had 10 players out against the Hawks on Monday night, the second night of a back-to-back, citing a combination of rest and injury management.)

But the games aren't slowing down for anyone. The season has become streamlined to some degree, with the shortening of training camp/preseason (the typical ramp-up period) and the insertion of the NBA Cup. It's clearly taken a toll on the players, which in turn takes a toll on the overall product. And there doesn't seem to be an immediate fix for cramming 82 games into a six-month schedule.

Rockets guard Marcus Smart, who's dealt with serious injuries over his 12-year career, had an honest take on the growing epidemic.

"The level of basketball has become so extreme on the human body and we don't have enough time to recover like we used to," Smart said. "When I first came in the league, training camp and preseason were damn near three weeks, multiple games to get your body ready for an 82-game season. Now, depending on how far you go in the playoffs, you might get two months off? It's tough on the body.

"People don't understand; you're flying, your body's inflamed, you're landing in different time zones, go to sleep late and gotta be up early. Run as hard as you can, jump as high and fast as you can and be as physical as you can. All you're doing is asking yourself for injuries. But we signed up for it, and have to figure out a way to minimize it. Some of our best players are getting hurt and it's very unfortunate. We all want to be out here doing what we love."

There is a long list of sports scientists, doctors, data analysts and professionals who will attempt to dissect such a rapid rise of injuries in a short amount of time. But simply watching every night, the games look more rigorous, more demanding and more taxing.

Possessions are up, with the Chicago Bulls topping out at 110.2 a night, up from 107 courtesy of the 2025-26 Miami Heat. League average pace from the previous campaign is now hovering over the bottom third. The game has also become more aggressive. Last season's Pistons team, thought to be one of the more physical units around, committed 22 fouls per game. That same figure would rank 18th today. More teams are comfortable sending bodies to the offensive glass, with the Milwaukee Bucks' 33 chances per night a whopping 10 marks higher than last season's No. 1. On the defensive end, no team averaged double digits in contested rebound rate last season — there are eight such teams now. Average team speed and distance are up, too.

"There's not just one straightforward answer," said Jeff Stotts, injury analyst and owner of InStreetClothes.com. "I think you're seeing younger players get injured with a higher frequency because they're likely to have more miles on their legs before they get to the NBA. … I think the game has outpaced the player. And now you're asking your bigger, stronger athletes to also cover more ground, not only work on accelerators but also their brakes. Asking our big guys to stretch their games all the way out to the 3-point line. All those things increase the chances and likelihood of injuries occurring along the way."

Last season, only three players averaged more than 36 minutes a night — Amen Thompson, Kevin Durant and Luka Dončić. That list has since nearly tripled in size. The aforementioned list of injured stars sans Haliburton also has another thing in common: all of their usage rates were lower than the previous season. What that means is while you may be on the ball less, you're off the ball more — relocating, cutting, changing direction and using your body in other ways that still can accumulate pressure and load.

A good indicator of injury prevention is measuring an athlete's workload against their overall fitness level. Essentially the more they're asked to do physically, the more time they need to recover.

"We're in this constant back and forth between rest and acute-to-chronic load ratio, where we're trying to minimize spikes," Stotts said. "Sudden increases in load on the body in relationship to the overall conditioning of the athlete. The teams that have found success have found ways to quantify the data in a way that helps buy-in. Some teams won't rest players; others will build it in. There's no great cure."

Is such a spike in absences this early into the season enough to warrant an alternative look at scheduling or the larger umbrella of how the sport is played altogether? No magic wand can guarantee a clean bill of health, but the games, pace and aggression are increasing in frequency — and the players, whom the entire business model and ecosystem revolves around, are suffering because of it.

Track funnel performance and flag drop-off points to the pod lead you're supporting

Run structured experiments (creative, audience, incentive, or timing) with clear hypotheses

Turn what works into documented playbooks the next person can pick up

Keep data quality and campaign tracking clean enough that leadership can trust the numbers

Repeat Phones: Reactivation rate, upgrade conversion %, unit economics per sale

Digital Lending: Approval → disbursement rate, digital loan volume, approval quality

Campaign ROI and payback period across both

💼 Tech Stack: CleverTap (primary), Looker Channels: WhatsApp, SMS, push, in-app messaging

5+ years in growth, performance, or lifecycle marketing, ideally in fintech, telco, or e-commerce, with real ownership over what a campaign or a pod achieved, not just involvement in it.

Judgment as much as skill. You can walk into a pod, understand what's working and why, and make calls on where to push, not just follow a brief.

Genuine appetite for pod leadership. This is a deliberate path toward owning a pod outright, and the breadth here is designed to get you there faster than a single-channel role would.

Hands-on fluency in CRM platforms like CleverTap, Braze, or similar, and comfortable reading a funnel or cohort without translation.

Energized, not unsettled, by ambiguity. You'll work across two pods and land in a third decision point by mid-year. If that sounds like an opportunity rather than a risk, you'll thrive here.

Strong enough presence to build trust quickly with senior leads across two different pods.

Google dismantles botnet secretly running on 1.3 million Android TV boxes

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Google has cut ties with the company behind the botnet campaign.

Google’s Threat Intelligence Group says it has shut down a massive botnet called x.cpe. At its height, x.cpe ran on more than 1.3 million Android TV devices, and unusually, these were Google-certified boxes. Google says its screening process missed the malware due to “obfuscation techniques” employed by Shenzhen-based Videostrong Technology.

In a detailed blog post, the Threat Intelligence Group explains how the x.cpe botnet evolved quickly from a single-stage dropper into a modular three-stage malware platform that sold access to third parties. Manufacturers must have their devices certified if they want access to Google apps and branding, a process that is supposed to catch threats like this. However, Videostrong’s compromised boxes, mostly sold under the MECOOL brand, evaded detection by using a backdoor to remotely install the malware after the devices were set up and online.

After devices appeared online, a stub application called x.cpe or com.oemservice would phone home. This package did not have any malicious capabilities of its own, but it had the system-level permissions necessary to install malware modules at the operator’s behest. It also disabled Play Protect, which would have otherwise flagged the silently installed packages as malicious.

Google found most of the boxes were running residential proxies for routing large volumes of nefarious traffic or modules that engaged in ad fraud schemes. Selling access to this botnet was probably a big business. Videostrong also had full access to the devices, allowing operators to run terminal commands, capture the screen, and probe other devices on the local network. Google reportedly observed Videostrong doing this in real time.

Google worked with several other organizations to disrupt the x.cpe network, including Japan’s National Police Agency, Synthient, and Trend Micro. Google says it has taken legal action to shut down the domains and servers Videostrong used to operate and install its malware. It also went after command-and-control servers operated by Videostrong that were connected to other malware campaigns. Google estimates Videostrong has been cut off from about 10 million compromised devices.

This action should make the affected devices safe to use, but probably not as safe as they should be. Play Protect is still disabled, and the inert malware modules are still installed. Since they are loaded as system apps, users cannot easily remove them.

Synthient has published a list of 182 device model names known to be included in the botnet. Anyone who has purchased a MECOOL or white label Android TV box of unknown origin should check it. Trend Micro counted a total of 1,472,554 infected devices, somewhat higher than Google’s estimate. The overwhelming majority were in Brazil, Malaysia, Iraq, Mexico, and Taiwan, but tens of thousands were also online in the US, Germany, the UK, Australia, and Canada.

Google determined the operation was not the work of a rogue actor, so it has ended its relationship with Videostrong. The company, as well as any subsidiaries, will not be permitted to use Google’s Android apps and services.

Google also notes that it is updating its certification process to close the loophole that allowed Videostrong to operate its botnet on certified devices. Android is open source, though, and there are countless uncertified Android-based TV boxes out there. Videostrong could continue to sell malicious hardware under other brands without Google apps. But it would still have to rebuild its malware operation from scratch.

With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

How Tourism Ireland Is Navigating a More Uncertain Travel Market: Watch Video

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Photo Credit: Skift Transatlantic Summit 2026

At the Skift Transatlantic Summit, Tourism Ireland Chief Executive Alice Mansergh explained how the island of Ireland is navigating current headwinds by investing in connectivity marketing, market diversification, AI-driven discovery, and the authentic local experiences that can keep Ireland high on travelers’ wish lists.

This sponsored content was created in collaboration with a Skift partner.

North America remains one of Ireland’s most valuable visitor markets, but the outlook is getting more complicated. At the Skift Transatlantic Summit, Tourism Ireland Chief Executive Alice Mansergh pointed to continued growth alongside signs of softening demand as travelers contend with higher costs and economic uncertainty.

So far in 2026, Ireland has welcomed 5% more visitors from North America, with spending up 10%. But Mansergh said the past few months have been softer, as travelers think more carefully about their finances and increasingly seek flexible or refundable bookings.

“For all of us, it becomes really important to be at the top of the travel wishlist,” she said.

That challenge is shaping Tourism Ireland’s marketing strategy. Its new “Ireland Goes Beyond” campaign highlights the scenery, culture, heritage, and local experiences that make Ireland worth choosing, even as travelers become more selective about where they spend.

Staying on travelers’ wish lists is only part of the challenge. Ireland also needs to maintain the air connections that bring those travelers to the destination. With jet fuel costs up sharply and fewer Europeans traveling to the U.S., Mansergh said airlines are looking more closely at which transatlantic routes remain profitable.

Tourism Ireland works with carriers to promote strategic routes and stimulate inbound demand. Mansergh summed up the immediate challenge simply: “fill the planes to keep the planes.”

Healthy two-way travel matters, she added, because weaker outbound traffic from Europe can put the same routes that bring North American visitors to Ireland under pressure. “When you lose the connectivity, that can be damaging,” she said.

Those pressures reinforce the importance of a diversified visitor base. North America generates just over a third of Ireland’s inbound overseas revenue, with Europe contributing roughly another third. Britain offers another opportunity, with visitors from Ireland’s nearest neighbor twice as likely to return.

“Now is not the time to be wholly dependent on just one market or another,” Mansergh said.

Beyond traditional marketing and airline partnerships, Tourism Ireland is also adapting to how travelers discover destinations. Its research found that the number of people naming AI as their primary source of travel inspiration has doubled in the past year.

The organization is responding by optimizing its digital content for generative AI. According to Mansergh, information provided by Tourism Ireland now contributes to answers for 19% of queries about the destination across tools including ChatGPT, Claude, and Gemini.

The goal is to influence which experiences AI recommends, particularly lesser-known attractions and local businesses that might otherwise be overlooked.

But visibility in AI search also depends on what travelers are seeing and saying about a destination across the web. “Choose what you want to be famous for,” Mansergh said.

Tourism Ireland has applied that thinking to Halloween, building awareness of the holiday’s roots in the ancient Irish festival of Samhain through its own content and platforms such as Reddit, YouTube, and Tripadvisor.

“You need a bit of a surround sound of people talking about the things you want them to be talking about,” she said.

Technology may be changing how travelers discover Ireland, but the experiences that attract them often have little to do with technology.

“For me, I always want AI to stand for, yes, artificial intelligence, but also authentic Ireland,” she said.

She pointed to sheepdog demonstrations, cider-making farms, and traditional currach-building as examples of experiences that bring travelers closer to local culture while spreading tourism to more regions.

“The more digital we are,” Mansergh said, “the more we crave those authentic touch and feel real moments.”

For 2027, Tourism Ireland plans to keep focusing on connectivity, year-round travel, and emerging opportunities such as “cool-cations.” Taken together, those priorities reflect Tourism Ireland’s approach to a more uncertain market: Protect the connections that bring visitors to Ireland, reach travelers through new channels, and give them compelling reasons to visit.

Discover how Tourism Ireland is inspiring travelers to explore more of the island of Ireland. Learn more about Tourism Ireland’s global marketing initiatives.

Bill & Ted Sidescroller to Launch Backerkit Campaign in 2027

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

For the uninitiated, Bill & Ted’s Excellent Adventure was a 1989 sci-fi comedy starring Alex Winter and Keanu Reeves as the titular duo of teenage weirdos who get a chance to travel through time, meet famous historical figures, and make mild nuisances of themselves. It also co-starred my personal hero and one of the greatest comedians of all time, the late George Carlin, as their host in a time-traveling phone booth, Rufus.

The film, as well as its two sequels, remains one of the hallmarks of late 80s, early 90s pop culture, with any proclamation of “excellent” being a great way to make millennials spontaneously start air guitar-ing. Outside its films, the IP pops up in mildly odd places every now and then, including, apparently, a brand-new video game.

Spider-Man's video game adaptations are just as wild and varied as his comic book counterpart's adventures.

Indie developer Art of Play Interactive announced today that it’s developing a brand-new Bill & Ted game, Bill & Ted’s Excellent Adventure Arcade, a hybrid sidescrolling beat ‘em up and minigame collection based on the first film. This game is slated to be bankrolled by a Backerkit campaign, which is planned to launch in February 2027 with a handful of physical rewards available for early backers, as well as presently unannounced other swag like collectibles and apparel.

According to the game’s Backerkit and Steam pages, Bill & Ted’s Excellent Adventure Arcade is meant to be a playable version of the original film, starting with Rufus whisking the boys away into history in the time-traveling phone booth and running them through 15 levels as they try to meet famous folks for their history report. The game will feature audio from Alex Winter, Keanu Reeves, and George Carlin ripped directly from the film’s original recordings. It’s all original stuff, by the way; the Backerkit page stresses that this project will not use any generative AI whatsoever.

Bill & Ted’s Excellent Adventure Arcade is meant to be a playable version of the original film.

The actual game seems to switch off between side-scrolling beat ‘em up and assorted minigames. Bill and Ted travel through the time periods, getting into scuffles or running away from them, and when they encounter a famous historical figure, you play minigames to earn their trust and get them to come with you, such as battling Beethoven in a music duel or beating Napoleon in an ice cream-eating contest.

Sébastien Benard's action roguelike has resurfaced with a release window after several years of radio silence.

The game is meant to be played in local co-op, one character per player, though you can also play it solo and assume control over both Bill and Ted simultaneously. After beating the game’s main story, you’ll unlock arcade mode for faster playthroughs, as well as the individual mini-games for piecemeal enjoyment.

As you often see for these kinds of campaigns, and especially those for popular IPs from the 80s, the Backerkit campaign for Bill & Ted’s Excellent Adventure Arcade is planned to be accompanied by assorted physical rewards for those who get in on the ground floor.

To start, everyone who backs the campaign will receive a free Rufus Metallic Phone Booth card; those who reserve their pledge for $5 will receive additional Bill and Ted metallic cards, and everyone who backs in the first 48 hours will receive a fourth metallic card featuring the “historical dudes.” These four cards can be built together with the included top and bottom pieces to create your own little phone booth model. According to the Backerkit page, there will be additional incentives during the campaign proper, including exclusive merch, apparel, and other goodies.

The game is aiming for digital release on Nintendo Switch, PlayStation 5, Xbox Series X/S, and PC via Steam. Additionally, the game will have a limited physical release, with boxes for the Switch and PS5 versions, and even a physical CD-ROM for the PC version. There also seems to be a knock-off SNES cartridge in the works, though I’m assuming it’s just a prop and won’t actually run on a SNES.

Open-world games are the norm for AAA developers, but games funded on Kickstarter can deliver immersive open-worlds too.

Why Thinking Rationally Often Doesn’t Seem to Help

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 3, 2026 | Reviewed by Tyler Woods

Thinking rationally is often presented as a magic answer to life’s problems. If we can just think more rationally and understand the reality of our world, then we will feel much better.

At least that’s what people say. But the truth is, just recognizing the reality of what is happening to us and around us often is not enough. We can understand clearly what is happening and still feel lousy about it.

Take the following example. A person recognizes that their job is no longer working for them. They go into work every day, put in the hours and feel absolutely terrible. After thinking about it, they realize that they hate their job. It may be that the work is no longer meaningful, they don’t feel like they are going anywhere, their boss is a tyrant, or a combination of all three. Coming to the conclusion, rationally, that the source of their distress is the meaninglessness or difficulty of their job can be useful.

But notice how recognizing reality does not guide the person on what to do here. It does not tell the person whether to leave their job, talk with their boss, or find some way to manage the problems the job presents. It also offers no guidance on how to handle the stress, as the person remains at the job even if they decide to look for a new one.

We often expect that if we just understand the reality of a situation, we will be able to deal with it or know what to do with it. But that is not the way reality actually works. Recognizing that something “is the way it is”, to quote an overused cliche, does not mean that we will know how to handle that reality.

Our experiences are ones where we all have to deal with the reality of our social world. But reality itself does not present information about what meaning we associate with it. Things happen, situations evolve, and people act as they do without these situations providing any clear guidance on how we are supposed to respond.

I once had a conversation with an expert in biological evolution who used a phrase that I still use today: “Evolution isn't good or bad, it just is.” I think about that phrase whenever I reflect on the fact that the world itself does not provide any clear indication of what things mean when they occur. All we know about the world is that things happen, and that things are the way they are, but knowing that rationally does not tell us what it means.

Rational thinking is a good starting place for handling reality effectively. But it is not enough. In order to effectively use rational information, you need to incorporate your own thinking about values and meaning.

In some ways, this is an extension of rational thinking. It is just rational thinking turned more inward. You move from asking “what facts do I know about what is happening here?” to “what facts do I know about how I define meaning and purpose?” Knowing those facts about how you want to live your life allows you to incorporate them into processing effectively what is happening to you and what is happening around you.

So, the solution is not to move away from thinking rationally. It is living your life more rationally. Living in a rational way does not mean you know the facts about what is happening to you and around you. It also means knowing:

Here are some important points to consider when trying to live your life in a more fully rational way

Anthropic is still grappling with the economics of compute

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

There’s a lot of mystery around the finances of major AI labs, and as Anthropic inches towards its IPO, we’re starting to get some answers. In particular, the company is finally disclosing more about the economics of compute, and how much it of Anthropic’s skyrocketing revenue is being lost to compute costs.

In a new filing on Monday, Anthropic released data that showed those margins on compute actually getting worse. The filing was an update to the S-1 released last week — but where that filing’s financial disclosures only covered through June 30th of this year, the new data extended to disclose results through an extra quarter, to September 30th. Broadly, Anthropic had a good quarter: revenue reached $17 billion, up from $11.5 billion in the previous quarter, suggesting durable growth. But the operating loss grew at the same time, thanks in large part to growing compute costs.

Looking at the compute margins makes the picture even worse. In the new quarter, compute took up 58% of the company’s revenue, up from 48% the quarter before. At a moment when the company could win over IPO investors by showing margins improving, they’re getting worse.

A chart in the filing shows this even more clearly, tracking the company’s revenue for every dollar spent on compute. Since the start of 2025, it’s been steadily growing, peaking at just over 2x this past quarter, then falling back down to 1.72x

Many indications imply that this shift in compute margins is more of a blip than a long-term problem, but it still tells us important things about the business of running a major lab. Trying to keep up with demand, Anthropic’s compute bill shot up to $10 billion for the quarter, including, as the filing notes, “$5.0 billion of compute under short-term commitments, which currently costs significantly more than comparable compute procured under our multi-year commitments.” Basically, demand was growing so fast that Anthropic had to scramble for compute, which meant paying higher rates.

Some of those deals have already played out in public. Anthropic has been paying SpaceX $1.25 billion a month for compute, alongside multi-billion dollar deals with Nscale, Akamai and Volta. In the parlance of the industry, these are neoclouds rather than hyperscalers — smaller outfits that offer more flexible compute deals at higher rates.

Anthropic’s margins got worse in Q3 because of deals like these, and there’s no sign they’ll be able to do without them anytime soon. As the new filing puts it, “We expect compute and infrastructure expenses to increase in absolute dollars and remain our largest operating expense for the foreseeable future as additional committed compute becomes available to us under multi-year arrangements with CSPs, and as we continue to invest in additional compute.”

Anthropic is still trying to lower its compute costs. In particular, the company talks quite a bit about its efforts to build its own compute base under a project it calls “Anthropic Direct.” But with demand growing so fast, those efforts aren’t going to be able to keep pace, and the neoclouds will continue to be the best option they have.

In a sense, these are champagne problems. As long as revenue is growing at nearly 50% a quarter, few investors will worry about high compute costs — and the moment demand slows down, investors may be comforted by a compute picture that has gotten a lot sunnier. But it’s a reminder of how much AI labs still depend on the trillion-dollar infrastructure buildout — and how vulnerable they are to hiccups in that process. It’s easy to imagine the data center buildout slowing down over the next year if bond markets get skittish or local opposition gets more intense. There’s not much Anthropic can do to address either of those risks, and either one would make a messy compute picture a whole lot messier.

In the meantime, the future of Anthropic and other labs will be at the mercy of compute prices.

Mariners hire Cubs executive with no managerial experience as new manager

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

After a colossally disappointing 2026 season, the Seattle Mariners are bringing in a first-time manager. The Mariners have named Chicago Cubs director of player development Jason Kanzler their new manager, the team announced Tuesday. Seattle fired manager Dan Wilson after the season.

"Our goal in this process was finding a manager with the ability to challenge all of us to improve," president of baseball operations Jerry Dipoto said in a statement. "Jason has the mindset, expertise, communication skills, and intensity to help create a championship standard here in Seattle."

The Mariners will be Kanzler's first managerial job at any level. At 36, he will be the second-youngest manager in baseball, older than only 34-year-old Washington Nationals skipper Blake Butera.

Bringing in a manager with zero previous managerial experience at any level is a rare move, certainly, though it is not unprecedented. Most notably, the New York Yankees hired Aaron Boone straight from the broadcasting booth in 2018. Boone had no managerial or coaching experience at any level. He's led the Yankees to the postseason eight times in nine seasons.

"I am thrilled to join the Mariners organization and I'm looking forward to hitting the ground running," Kanzler said in a statement. "This team has an immense amount of talent in all facets of the game, and my coaching staff and I are excited to access and unlock this team's full potential."

Kanzler may have no prior managerial experience, but this will not be his first time on a big-league staff. He was a major-league coach with the Houston Astros in 2022 and their assistant hitting coach in 2023, and had coached in their farm system previously. The Cubs hired Kanzler to oversee their player development in 2024.

Former Astros manager Joe Espada was reportedly the other finalist for the Mariners job.

The Mariners went 76-86 this past season and finished five games back in the woebegone AL West. A year ago, they went to Game 7 of the ALCS and were five outs away from their first-ever trip to the World Series. Seattle entered 2026 as a trendy World Series pick and instead fell flat, mostly because so many core hitters underperformed.

Kanzler will be the team's third manager since Dipoto was hired to run baseball operations in September 2015, joining Scott Servais (2015-24) and Wilson (2024-26).

This Mid-90s Austrian Car Company Cobbled Together A Lineup With A Lightly Rebadged Ford, Mercury, Saturn And Dodge

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sauber isn't a strange way to say Saab, also a defunct auto brand. Nor is it in any way related to the Sauber racing team that finally closed shop in 2025, giving way for Audi to take a seat at the Formula 1 table. The Sauber we are talking about today was an extremely short-lived Austrian car brand that existed from 1993-1995, solely to import American cars to Austria to sell them under the Sauber name. Well, it was a little more complicated than that. Its relative obscurity sent YouTuber corsicar on one of the most amusing missions of discovering what exactly the brand was, and more importantly, "why?"

Prior to corsicar's deep dive into this mysterious car company, knowledge on Sauber had been little to none. It seemed to have made it into the curious car chasm in 2025 via TheTruthAboutCars, and an Autopost article done shortly after. One simply cannot internet search Google themselves into a hole for hours taking in information on Sauber otherwise. Coriscar, however, found a newspaper ad for a vehicle called a Sauber Villager. It looks strangely similar to Mercury's Villager. Almost identical, because it is the same vehicle. This gets the whole search rolling.

The Sauber brand was established by a married couple in Austria in 1993. After getting things situated in Austria, the owners founded another company in the U.S., in Florida, Sauber Automobiles USA, Inc. This was likely to get around import laws and accruing unwanted taxes.

According to a review Coriscar found of the Sauber Villager in Auto Revue magazine, the Florida office was used to purchase American vehicles that would then be registered in the U.S. From there, it could export those vehicles to Austria, where they'd get a light facelift to make changes to meet Austrian regulations, and with a little rebadging, they'd officially be "Sauber" cars, as demonstrated by the ads found and shared in the video.

The company essentially tried to make a complete lineup of in-demand or soon-to-be in-demand vehicles using different American market models. There was the family hauler, minivan or MPV Sauber Villager, which was the Mercury Villager, a Ford product, which was also a Nissan product. Sauber's mid-size sedan, coupe and or wagon, the Messager, was General Motors' Saturn S-class sedan, 2-door coupe, and wagon. The entry-level econo-car was the unfortunately named Sauber Teenager, or a Ford Aspire. And the sporty car was the Sauber S1, which was a Dodge Stealth or Mitsubishi 3000GT.

Sauber models went on sale in 1994 with 12 existing dealers. The hope was to expand to 30 by mid-year. At those dealers, interested buyers could consider the cheaper, poorly named Teenager at around $14,500 (in 1994), the Messager at $22,000, the base Villager at $41,600, its top-of-the-line First Class trim level cost around $54,700, and the S1 offered at $76,100. Just for kicks, to give you an idea of how much that costs today with inflation, the Teenager would start at $33,000 and the top of the line, sporty S1 would be just shy of $175,000.

To get the attention of potential buyers, Sauber really played with the Austrian sense of humor. One ad shown in the video reads (in Austrian) "In life you have to try everything at least once. A test drive in a Sauber doesn't hurt either." Pictured below the text was a Sauber Villager (or basically edited Mercury Villager), and above the text, a pair of boxing gloves, cigars, and a leather crotch harness for you know exactly what.

It seems the ads were not funny enough because in May 1995 documents show the Commercial Court of Vienna rejected the car company's intent to declare bankruptcy, and instead officially declared the company dissolved. Sauber was so broke, it had nothing to help pay off debtors in a bankruptcy. What ruined it isn't clear. Importing cars from another country is an expensive endeavor. Buying a car to then make costly adjustments to them to meet regulations likely didn't help either. The whole model doesn't feel very viable. It also happens that a new tax introduced in 1994 — the Austrian Value-Added Tax Act — would have subjected those imports to heavier taxation.

Also, spending $100,000 (today) on a minivan is outrageous. I can't imagine the pricing did them any favors when there were plenty of vehicles in Europe, as well as imported for a price that was much more palatable for European families.

The whole thing almost doesn't seem real, but coriscar prudently shared every bit of archived paperwork, magazines, and ads he finds to verify that Sauber was indeed real, including a 1994 review of the Villager in Austria's Auto Revue. He actually bought almost all of the Auto Revues he could find just to find this one real-life review.

Corsicar was also able to find out through extensive research that the brand had maybe sold 28 cars in that time. In reality, it's still more real than Tesla's lastest Roadster might ever be.

If you do have any bit of a soul, the last part of corsicar's video will tug at your heartstrings. The YouTuber looked into how many of these nearly extinct relics had stayed on the road by looking at Sauber registrations in Austria. The last one on the road was in 2018. By 2019, not a single Sauber remained in motion — at least in Austria. If one has survived, we'll only know if you post a photo of it in the comments.

Epidemic of Hacked Freezers Spreads Across Europe

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Epidemic of Hacked Freezers Spreads Across Europe

Europe's freezers have gone rogue, with supermarkets in nine more countries reporting refrigeration systems that heated up overnight on Monday.

Stores in Belgium, Estonia, Finland, Ireland, Italy, Luxembourg, the Netherlands, Portugal and Sweden reported problems, and Estonian units were pushed to 50°C (122 °F).

No group has claimed responsibility, so the only ones taking the heat are the freezers.

The outbreak has a patient zero in France, where Finance Minister Roland Lescure told Parliament on Oct. 13 that about 700 of the country's 10,000 supermarkets were affected.

According to France's CERT-FR, hackers forced a continuous defrost cycle, which spoiled food and sparked fires at some stores. CERT-FR said it was aware of numerous compromises of remote control modules made by Danfoss and Copeland.

Groupement Mousquetaires, which owns Intermarché and Netto, counted 300 stores affected, while Cooperative U said 80 of its 2,000 shops were hit. Lescure said food supplies were not at risk but acknowledged the attack "touches the heart of our food security." Some Mousquetaires display cases were still out of service that Tuesday.

Monday's wave hit several chains in Belgium, where Carrefour reported disruption at about 15 of its 700 stores. Sabcobel, a Belgian refrigeration contractor, said evaporators had been switched into defrost mode, and federal prosecutors opened an investigation. Four Delhaize outlets in Luxembourg also reported problems.

Italy's Radenza Group, which runs Coop-branded stores in Sicily, said the attack began at 4 a.m local time. and targeted the remote-control system for frozen food sections. Ireland's Musgrave counted 13 SuperValu stores affected, and Mosqueteiros confirmed incidents at seven Intermarché stores in Portugal.

At 50°C (122 °F), a freezer is well past hot-tub territory and turns basically into a low-powered steam oven or, worse, a bacteria incubator where germs grow quickly if food is left there too long.

In Sweden, Coop said six of its roughly 800 stores were hit and that they ran on 4G connections, making them "more exposed." Ica said a limited number of stores had problems and that the affected shops used an external Danfoss control system. Neither chain has confirmed a cyberattack, and Finland's Kesko says network interference has not been ruled out.

EU Agriculture Commissioner Christophe Hansen called the incidents a "deliberate attempt" to disrupt the food supply chain. Investigators have not said whether Monday's wave is linked to the French attack.

Estonian refrigeration firm Kitman Coldtech said the affected units were older Danfoss models similar to those attacked in France. CERT-FR warns that leaving an end-of-life device without security patches exposed to the internet carries a very high risk of compromise. Researchers at Claroty published flaws in Danfoss AK-SM 800A controllers in August, including an authentication bypass.

Claroty found 2,765 of those devices exposed online, and Danfoss fixed the flaws in firmware R4.3.1. It remains unclear which weaknesses the attackers used, and CERT-FR says the campaigns are probably still ongoing, while Italy's government was set to hold a cybersecurity meeting on Tuesday.

For now, CERT-FR advises operators to take the controllers off the public internet, and Sabcobel's CEO says many older units need physical modifications that require investment. Until those arrive, there is no real fix for the oldest units, which makes your 1998 chest freezer the most secure device on the continent.

Meta pays up to borrow $15B as investors grow wary of AI debt

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Its 30-year bonds pay well above the deal it sold in April, as Big Tech floods the market with debt.

Meta is selling at least $15B of dollar bonds in a deal expected to reach at least $20B with a first euro note, Bloomberg reports. It is offering much higher yields than in April, as investors balk at Big Tech’s flood of AI debt.

Meta is selling at least $15B of US dollar bonds, its first debt sale in six months, Bloomberg reported on Tuesday, citing people familiar with the matter.

The two-currency deal is expected to raise at least $20B in total. It includes Meta’s first euro-denominated note, which could be sold as soon as Wednesday.

Meta is paying more to draw buyers. Its 30-year bonds are being discussed at about 2.5 percentage points above US government debt. That is nearly 0.9 points more than the early pricing on a similar bond Meta sold in April.

“We see no need to chase it in the new issue market,” said Ross Pamphilon of Impax Asset Management. He expects Meta and its rivals to keep spending heavily until at least 2027.

Big Tech has sold almost $430B of high-grade bonds since last year. Their bonds now trade at the widest gap over other high-grade debt since 2003. Alphabet paid up for a €6B euro sale on Monday, after Microsoft’s $25B deal last week.

Meta is also selling shares. Last week it priced $25B of common stock and $12.5B of preferred shares, part of its record equity raise.

The bond sale follows S&P’s move on Friday to cut its outlook on Meta’s credit rating over its AI spending.

Microsoft has listed some concrete ways that Windows 11 is getting better with 8GB of RAM

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You should start noticing improved performance in a wide range of apps.

Microsoft has been doing a whole lot of shouting recently about how its 8GB Windows 11 PCs are performing better than ever, and it's now shared some more specific information about where you might start to notice a difference.

As previously revealed, the company claims that 8GB PCs are now up to 20 percent more responsive and 40 percent faster at multitasking. Windows is now able to retain up to 2GB more of your active data, rather than moving it to slower storage, which helps your PC stay more responsive as more tabs appear in your browser.

Since January, Microsoft says that interactions like opening Start, File Explorer and Settings, as well as searching and right-clicking, are all up to 20 percent more responsive, which it has achieved by directing your computer to reserve tasks like search indexing while you're working. Apps like Edge can also open up to 33 percent faster, while Efficiency Mode in Teams is turned on by default on 8GB PCs. This can speed up launch by up to 20 percent by dynamically reducing the load on your system resources.

Microsoft also says it has improved multitasking workflow speeds by up to 40 percent, which it tested by opening 20 tabs in Edge, as well as a 4K video, the Outlook app, File Explorer, and large files in Word PowerPoint and Excel. These were all left running once opened, and while 16GB of memory or more is still recommended for processor-hungry creatives who can afford it, you should be able to get more done quickly on a humble 8GB PC.

Apps like Word, PowerPoint, Excel and Outlook now use up to 360MB less RAM, which Microsoft says is down to improved efficiency, and even third-party apps like WhatsApp are hogging less memory than before. Other RAM-saving methods include Widgets that only load when opened and inactive tabs going to sleep after 15 minutes by default on 8GB configurations.

Aside from Windows itself, Microsoft says that newer processors are already playing their part. For example, a 2026 8GB Dell XPS 13 delivers over 90 percent faster multi-core CPU performance than its 2021 predecessor. The same 8GB model was able to largely match a 2021 configuration with twice as much memory in general CPU performance, web browsing, 4K video export and some AI tasks.

These performance and memory efficiency improvements require your Windows 11 PC to be running version 26H2 on the build 26300.9818 or later. Microsoft also recommends keeping all your apps updated and sometimes these improvements roll out through them.

‘La Bola Negra’ Becomes Spain’s Third Biggest Spanish Hit Ever as Its Box Office Surges 8% Above Pre-Pandemic Average

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Rentrak figures unveiled at Valladolid’s Merci market show indie distributors gaining share as cinema admissions rise 29% through September

Javier Calvo and Javier Ambrossi’s “La Bola Negra” has become the third highest-grossing Spanish film of all time at Spain’s domestic box office, earning an estimated €36.7 million ($41.0 million) through Nov. 1, according to Rentrak figures.

Only Emilio Martínez-Lázaro’s blockbuster comedy “Ocho apellidos vascos” (“Spanish Affair,” $61.8 million) and J.A. Bayona’s disaster survival drama “Lo imposible” (“The Impossible,” $47.3 million) have generated more theatrical revenue among Spanish productions. Related Stories Netflix Touts International Success Amid Content Leadership Shake-Up, Eyes More Broadcaster Deals After TF1 and Globo Pacts 'KPop Demon Hunters,' 'Squid Game' Stars Fete Netflix's Korea Decade as Ted Sarandos Teases 'Tantara,' 'All of Us Are Dead' Season 2

Distributed by independent outfit Elastica Films, the Federico García Lorca-inspired drama, starring Penélope Cruz and Glenn Close, has sold around 5.4 million tickets, emerging as Spain’s biggest homegrown hit of 2026.

Its success comes amid a sharp recovery for the Spanish theatrical market. Box office revenue surged 40% year-on-year in the first 41 weeks of 2026, reaching €489.4 million ($544.8 million) through Oct. 11, according to figures presented by Rentrak at Valladolid’s Merci independent film market.

That puts Spanish box office revenue 8% above the comparable pre-pandemic average for 2017-2019.

Spain has also outperformed the global market. Through September, its box office was up 35% year-on-year, compared with an 11% increase worldwide.

‘La Bola Negra,’ ‘Torrente for President’ Deliver Major Hits

A significant part of Spain’s surge has come from homegrown movies.

“La Bola Negra,” Santiago Segura’s “Torrente for President” and animated feature “Tadeo Jones and the Magic Lamp” account for roughly 60% of Spanish films’ box office this year. That said, around a dozen domestic productions have also surpassed $1.1 million in theatrical revenue.

“La Bola Negra” has surpassed the lifetime domestic grosses of both “Ocho apellidos catalanes,” (“Spanish Affair 2”) the 2015 sequel to Spain’s biggest homegrown hit, and “Torrente Presidente,” another major box office success of 2026.

“Before its release, ‘La Bola Negra’ had all the ingredients to be a major hit, but there was no way to foresee the scale of its success,” Rentrak Spain general manager David Rodríguez said. “Above all, its directing duo is a recognized brand in its own right, capable of mobilizing audiences and capturing the latest social trends and sensibilities.”

Its ultimate box office potential remains difficult to gauge, Rodríguez added, although possible Goya and Oscar nominations could extend its theatrical run.

Rentrak’s Cinequest research sheds light on the film’s unusually broad appeal.

Among respondents surveyed about “La Bola Negra,” 39.2% were under 35, compared with 25.1% for dramas overall. Women represented 62.1%.

The film also led Cinequest’s rankings for converting advance audience interest into definite theatrical viewing intention among 55 titles distributed by members of independent distributors association Adicine.

Although Cinequest detected strong interest and viewing intentions before release, the eventual scale of the success exceeded what those indicators could predict.

“It’s brought in many more young people, who are talking about it among themselves. The word of mouth has been tremendous,” said Rentrak client insights manager Joaquín Vegas.

“Regardless of a film’s subject or genre, the secret is finding a good story that audiences can connect with and make their own,” he added.

Vegas also praised Elastica’s promotional campaign, which helped build awareness and interest ahead of the release.

Spain’s Five Highest-Grossing Spanish Films – By Nov. 1, 2026

“Spanish Affair” (2014) — €55.4 million ($61.8 million)

“The Impossible” (2012) — €42.4 million ($47.3 million)

“La Bola Negra” (2026) — €36.7 million ($41.0 million)

“Spanish Affair 2” (2015) — €35.5 million ($39.6 million)

“Torrente for President” (2026) — €28.1 million ($31.4 million)

For Rodríguez, Spain’s recovery has been driven by a more consistent flow of box office hits throughout the year.

“After 2020, one film would take everything and the rest got nothing. Not anymore. The market is broadening,” he said.

Seven films have crossed the $22.3 million mark this year, while more than 50 have grossed over $2.2 million. Weekly box office receipts have regularly exceeded $11.1 million.

Hollywood blockbusters have helped drive the recovery. “Spider-Man: Brand New Day” and Christopher Nolan’s “The Odyssey” have attracted 5.4 million and five million spectators, respectively, in Spain.

Other major contributors include “Michael,” “The Super Mario Galaxy Movie” and “Toy Story 5.”

Despite the improving box office, cinema attendance has yet to return to pre-pandemic levels.

Admissions rose 29% between January and September compared with the same period in 2025, but remained 13% below the 2017-2019 average.

Cinequest suggests that much of the improvement comes from existing moviegoers attending more frequently, rather than from a substantial influx of new spectators.

“It’s not so much that people who didn’t go to the movies have started going, but that those who already went are going more often,” Vegas explained.

“There is life beyond Hollywood: almost 70% of global box office comes from outside North America, and local films are very important,” Rodríguez said.

For Rodríguez, the success of “La Bola Negra” also demonstrates the commercial potential of Spain’s independent distribution sector.

“Any independent distributor today is perfectly capable of handling major films and achieving fantastic box office results,” he said.

The improving market has brought substantial gains for members of Adicine, the association representing many of Spain’s leading independent film distributors.

Between January and September 2026, Adicine members generated $63.5 million in box office revenue, a 45% increase over the corresponding period in 2025.

Admissions rose 43% to 8.6 million, despite a 5% reduction in the number of films released.

Their combined box office market share increased from 11.5% to 12.4% over those same nine-month periods — figures that capture only the opening days of “La Bola Negra’s” theatrical run, before most of its remarkable October box office performance.

GTA 6 Pre-Load Times, Install Size, And Global Release Times Confirmed

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto 6Grand Theft Auto VIGTA 6Pre-Load

We’re just over two weeks out from the launch of Grand Theft Auto 6 and Rockstar Games has finally revealed all of the details people need to know about getting the digital-only open-world game installed on their gaming console of choice.

The studio also confirmed that, yes, GTA 6 has a whopping 212GB install size, so start making room for that and god help you if you have an Xbox Series S. Here’s everything you need to know about getting GTA 6 ready to play for its release on November 19, 2026.

Digital pre-loading on PlayStation 5 will start globally on Tuesday, November 10 at midnight local time. On Xbox Series X|S, pre-loading begins globally on Tuesday, November 10 at 12 a.m. GMT, which is 7:00 p.m. ET on the previous day, November 9.

If you pre-order GTA 6 digitally, you’ll have access to pre-load the game immediately. If you are waiting for one of the codes in a box to ship to your home or be picked up in store, those will become available starting November 12. You’ll be able to take that code, redeem it, and immediately start pre-loading the game.

That’s a big file size, and console servers are going to get hammered when everyone starts trying to download it at once. To put it in perspective, 212GB is roughly one third of the available storage on a base PS5 and roughly a quarter of the available storage on a 1TB model. On Xbox Series S, it’s closer to half. In addition to taking some time depending on the speed of your internet, you’ll want to make sure you have your library cleared of any non-essential games. Good luck to you if you have children who are constantly swapping between a dozen different games every day.

The global release times for when GTA 6 goes live were previously announced, and unfortunately the U.S. is one of the last time zones to get access to the game.

Yes, the game will have been out in some parts of the world for roughly 18 hours by the time GTA 6 goes live stateside. My advice is to just stay logged out that day.

USDC and USDT stolen, ETH swapped, XMR obscured: Inside a $106M EU hack

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

In an unexpected turn of events, a suspected theft of more than $106 million from a European Union [EU]-based entity has raised a fresh wave of concern in the crypto community.

According to pseudonymous blockchain investigator ZachXBT, on the 1st of November, an unidentified attacker drained funds from accounts held across multiple centralized cryptocurrency exchanges.

The suspected stolen assets included Circle [USDC] and Tether [USDT].

Following the suspected theft, the attacker reportedly began moving the funds through a series of transactions to make them harder to trace and potentially recover.

To begin with, the illicit actor initially swapped the stolen USDC and USDT into Ethereum [ETH]. The attacker then transferred the ETH to instant cryptocurrency exchanges, including WellWallet and Kyrrex.

Going forward, the wrongdoer converted ETH into Monero [XMR], a privacy-focused cryptocurrency. Since the latter uses privacy-enhancing technologies to conceal transaction details, including the sender, recipient, and transaction amount, tracing has become even more difficult.

Not only this, but the suspected hacker also reportedly bridged assets across multiple blockchain networks, adding another layer of complexity to the ongoing investigation.

However, ZachXBT offered an important revelation regarding the identity of the suspected attacker. Despite the scale of the incident and the laundering techniques involved, the investigator stated,

While the illicit actor remains unidentified, I can say with a high degree of confidence it is NOT Lazarus Group based on multiple laundering indicators.

However, the EU entity has not yet publicly confirmed the suspected theft, adding more suspicion to the aforementioned attack.

This comes on the heels of the European Banking Authority (EBA) recognizing USDC as the EU’s first major e-money token under MiCA.

At the same time, the European Securities and Markets Authority (ESMA) has directed national regulators to restrict services involving non-MiCA-compliant stablecoins to sell-only, conversion, transfer, and withdrawal functions, with continued services subject to strict supervision.

United Airlines will expand to northern Mexico next spring despite a cloudy outlook.

The Star Alliance carrier will return to Chihuahua Airport (CUU) in northern Mexico from Houston’s George Bush Intercontinental Airport (IAH) beginning March 28, 2027.

Edibray Gómez Gallegos, the secretary of tourism of Chihuahua state, said the new route will increase connectivity for the state and "reflects growing interest among North American travelers in discovering the many tourist attractions Chihuahua has to offer."

United last served the IAH-CUU route in 2020, according to schedule data from aviation analytics firm Cirium. It will join American Airlines among U.S. carriers serving the airport.

A spacious Bombardier CRJ-550 will be used on the route. The plane, originally a 70-seat CRJ-700, is fitted with 10 first-class, 20 extra-legroom economy and 20 economy seats.

Other news: United is bringing its nicest plane (with the Polaris Studios) to a city that never sees it

The new CUU route comes as airlines around the globe have been forced to adjust to soaring fuel costs.

United CEO Scott Kirby has previously warned that United has “no hesitation to cut capacity of flights that are losing money” in 2027 if fuel prices stay elevated as expected.

For context, the average global price for a gallon of jet fuel stood at $4.56 during the week ending Oct. 30, up 3.8% from the previous month's average, according to data from trade group IATA.

For now, however, the carrier remains mostly in growth mode. This new route is the latest in a bevy of additions from United in 2027.

The airline will add at least five new routes from San Francisco International Airport (SFO), including to Charleston International Airport (CHS) in South Carolina and Richmond International Airport (RIC) in Virginia, and from O’Hare International Airport (ORD) in Chicago to L.F. Wade International Airport (BDA) in Bermuda.

And then there is United’s blockbuster 10-route summer long-haul expansion that will add destinations including Luxembourg Airport (LUX) and Ljubljana Airport (LJU) in Slovenia to its map.

It is not all growth for United next year, though. The airline will end service to Tulum International Airport (TQO) in Mexico in January.

Overall, these additions and cuts underscore United's efforts to grow its network while keeping an eye on profitability.

Our Last Chance to Save This Country Begins Today. Let’s Not Waste It.

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Don’t even start thinking about the 2028 presidential election. Start thinking about what we’re going to do tomorrow and the day after that.

(Optional Video Accompaniment to This Post)

It is a decade now since the president came down the golden escalator and the country went straight to hell. All we've done as citizens of a self-governing democratic republic is squabble over the seating in the handbasket. Until Tuesday. Tuesday was nut-cutting time. Tuesday felt—and probably rightly—like the beginning of an extended last chance of which the country will have to spend every waking hour.

Just on Monday, the president closed out his demented road show with a weird address in front of one of his administration's most conspicuous blunders: the Reflecting Pool. Meanwhile, the Supreme Court, with his carefully manufactured conservative majority, gave the boot to his latest attempt to ratfck the California elections, and the Court did so without even the hint of an explanation. Then the president capped a rich, full day by bombing an Iranian island in the Persian Gulf, although I guess he went a little bananas on his little boutique website.

Mama said there'd be days like this. But she never said there'd be so many. And all the rest of them are on us. If I can offer one piece of advice it would be this: Ignore anything written that references the 2028 presidential election. If things break the way the polls say they will, there will be so much concentration required to do what we can to ameliorate the damage this administration has done and is capable of doing over the next two years. Starting the 2028 horse-race coverage this week would be yet another crime against poor, battered journalism. As the late Senator Edward Kennedy told a Democratic convention long ago:

Run through the tape, America. Finish the race.

‘The Cat in the Hat’ Review: Bill Hader Leads an Animated Dr. Seuss Comedy So Overstuffed It’s Like a Marvel Movie for Tots

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It's a mash-up that leaves the innocent wildness of Dr. Seuss in the dust.

When it came to devising the voice he would use for “Lincoln,” Daniel Day-Lewis spent months working out the precise timbre and inflection and rhythm of how he imagined Abraham Lincoln might have sounded. (The day he presented his Lincoln voice to Steven Spielberg, it felt momentous, as if the actor were unveiling a skyscraper he had designed.) All of which got me to thinking about the voice Daniel Day-Lewis might have used to play the Cat in the Hat. Reading the Dr. Seuss books as a kid, I always imagined the Cat in the Hat sounding vaguely aristocratic, a touch effete, with the edgy attitude of a blithe troublemaker. He was consciously obnoxious, though part of it is that he knew there was nothing really wrong with the mischief he was making. It was what every kid wanted and needed. Related Stories ‘The New Stanford Prison Experiment’ Creators Break Down Its Shocking Finale Netflix Stock Deflates to Two-Year Low. What Will It Take to Revive Revenue Growth?

We’ll never know how Daniel Day-Lewis would have voiced the Cat in the Hat. But I’m pretty sure that if he took on the role, he’d have made the Cat more spiky and audacious, less safe, more cut with personality, more…Cat in the Hat-like than Bill Hader does.

In the new digitally animated “The Cat in the Hat,” Hader, who is one of the film’s executive producers, doesn’t stumble into the black hole of misjudgment that Mike Myers did back in 2003, when he starred in a live-action “The Cat in the Hat” — a noisy, antic hairball of a movie, made in the shadow of Jim Carrey’s “How the Grinch Stole Christmas.” Myers gave the Cat a New Yawk snarl that made him sound like he’d come out of a ’60s Catskills resort. (I guess he figured if he could make a green ogre Scottish, he could do anything.) Myers, in his Cat costume, also looked like a marshmallow with teeth, which was more scary than inviting. Hader plays the Cat as capital-F Friendly, which “works,” but it’s also a bit of a letdown. You can feel the film working too hard to turn him into an ingratiating mascot.

I guess you could say that Alessandro Carloni and Erica Rivinoja, the writer-directors of “The Cat in the Hat,” have avoided the earlier version’s pitfalls. But only enough to make some major mistakes of their own. The new “Cat in the Hat,” it goes without saying at this point, makes no attempt to be a faithful adaptation of Dr. Seuss’s most fabled books. That would simply be too quaint for our buzzy, mercurial, attention-crashing, video-games-on-Ritalin era. Yet even as the movie departs all too eagerly from the innocent wildness of the Seuss books, it devotes itself, with a corporate diligence that’s the opposite of free-spirited, to mimicking the scale and spirit and plot setups of too many familiar blockbusters. From Pixar to “Minions” to “The Emoji Movie,” it’s a mish-mosh of pop recycling.

Thus, the teenage heroine, Gabby (voiced by Xochitl Gomez), who has just moved to a new city with her little brother, Sebastian (Tiago Martinez), and their single mom, Carmen (America Ferrera), is sad and lonely and alienated from her new surroundings in the exact mode of Riley, the heroine of “Inside Out.” Gabby is a trumpet player who doesn’t want to be a “band nerd” anymore. But when the Cat appears, looking to stir up some anarchic action, she sprays him with Mace. For a good while, he doesn’t exactly win over Gabby or Sebastian or their fussy British goldfish, Waffles (Matt Berry), whose ire at the Cat’s shenanigans is one of the few things the film preserves directly from the original book.

The family’s unpacked stuff includes three sealed-up cardboard boxes (one for every family member), and each of these packages of memorabilia becomes a literal portal to another world. We thought we were watching a “Cat in the Hat” movie, but we’re really watching a Marvel movie for tots, one that gets fused with a Pixar mash-up. The guiding spirit of “The Cat in the Hat” is bigger, louder, more erupting with gadgets and flimflam and paraphernalia, not to mention a glowy-eyed giant leaping demon monster.

At one point we meet Thing One and Thing Three, though not Thing Two (there’s a surprise reason he’s not there), the Cat in the Hat’s fabled small assistants, in their unruly blue-flame hair and red pajamas. They don’t speak — but in this movie, they communicate by clapping and clucking their tongues and tapping on themselves, as if they were the Bobby McFerrin percussion section. Are they fun? In a one-note meh sort of way. But as soon as the movie makes a meta joke about them having their own franchise, you realize where this is going. It turns out that there are more than three Things (there’s a whole clan of them), and if you suspect that the corporate powers that be told the filmmakers 1) you’re going to be moving merchandise, and 2) if you do it right, we’re talking the new Minions…the evidence for that is all over the screen. But these born-to-be-franchise-plush-toys are more cuddly than hilarious.

“The Cat in the Hat” is junior Marvel surrealism, but it’s also a de facto video-game movie dreamed up for a game that doesn’t exist: The Cat in the Hat Saves The Universe. The Cat starts out as an office drone, who works for a massive company that assigns him clients; his job is to perk up their spirits. He winds up raiding the Supply Closet, a veritable city of shelved detritus, all of which — even a grand piano — he stores in his hat, à la Mary Poppins’ magic carpet bag.

The Cat is an irresponsible worker who regularly racks up too high a score on the Mess-o-Meter. (Can you hear Dr. Seuss grinding his teeth?) But he has brought a singular object with him: a Go-Backer, which is a layered stopwatch that allows one to turn back time. Gabby thinks they can use it to go back to the hometown she misses. But first a plunge into those boxes, and a ride on their old red couch that turns into a protean motorized vehicle. They go under the sea, in an episode filled with creatures like a school of swimming underwear and an operatic whale with a plastic-rainbow unicorn horn. There’s a circus, a marching band playing “Shake It Off,” and other random diversions, all leading up to the battle with the monster known as the Something, who’s a gloss on Te Kā in “Moana.” All in all, I would give “The Cat in the Hat” about a 70 on the Mess-o-Meter — it “delivers,” but in a hectic, bumptious, overcrowded and underfelt way. It’s a kids’ fantasy that’s talking through its hat.
